How StepSecurity Harden Runner Detected Unexpected Microsoft Defender Installation on GitHub-hosted Ubuntu Runners

StepSecurity
2025-09-04 3 use cases 2 techniques 5 kill-chain phases detected

Microsoft Defender was unexpectedly installed on multiple workflow runs from mid-July through mid-August, causing abnormal network traffic. StepSecurity Harden Runner detected this infrastructure anomaly within hours, and GitHub Support has since resolved the issue

Actions on Objectives
Detected
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration.
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad).
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key. → [LLM] wdavdaemon or MDE Linux endpoints observed on CI/CD build runners
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking. → [LLM] CI/CD Linux build host outbound to gist.githubusercontent.com (tj-actions IOC pattern)
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage.
[LLM] wdavdaemon or MDE Linux endpoints observed on CI/CD build runners Installation Medium
Hunts for the Microsoft Defender daemon (wdavdaemon) executing on, or MDE cloud endpoints being contacted from, Linux hosts identified as CI/CD build runners. Mirrors the StepSecurity Harden-Runner anomaly that surfaced the unintended MDE deployment to GitHub-hosted Ubuntu runners (July–August 2025, GitHub issue actions/runner-images#12656). Build runners should not be running EDR; presence indicates either infrastructure drift, an unauthorised software deployment, or a supply-chain implant masquerading as MDE. Rationale: Anchors on the article's named process (wdavdaemon) and three exact MDE endpoints (x.cp.wd.microsoft.com, global.endpoint.security.microsoft.com, wdcp.microsoft.com), corroborated against Microsoft Learn's MDE-on-Linux docs (which also list cdn.x.cp.wd.microsoft.com, added here) and GitHub issue actions/runner-images#12656. Scope to CI/CD runner hostnames is what eliminates the obvious FP — wdavdaemon is legitimate everywhere except where MDE is not provisioned. The runnerPattern regex must be tuned per environment. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/actions/runner-images/issues/12656 • https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/defender-endpoint/microsoft-defender-endpoint-linux • https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/defender-endpoint/linux-support-connectivity
ATT&CKT1543.002 T1059.004
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
// CI/CD Linux build host running Microsoft Defender daemon (wdavdaemon) or
// contacting MDE cloud endpoints. Real build runners should not be running
// EDR — presence is infrastructure drift (GitHub Aug-2025) or implant.
let CIDevices = DeviceInfo
    | where Timestamp > ago(1d)
    | where OSPlatform == "Linux"
    | where DeviceName matches regex @"(?i)(runner|ci|build|gha|github-actions)"
         or MachineGroup matches regex @"(?i)(runner|ci|build|github)"
    | summarize make_set(DeviceId);
union isfuzzy=true
(   DeviceProcessEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
    | where DeviceId in (CIDevices)
    | where FileName =~ "wdavdaemon"
         or InitiatingProcessFileName =~ "wdavdaemon"
         or FolderPath has @"/opt/microsoft/mdatp/"
    | project Timestamp, DeviceName, FileName, FolderPath, ProcessCommandLine,
              InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, SHA256
),
(   DeviceNetworkEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
    | where DeviceId in (CIDevices)
    | where RemoteUrl has_any ("x.cp.wd.microsoft.com",
                                "global.endpoint.security.microsoft.com",
                                "wdcp.microsoft.com",
                                "cdn.x.cp.wd.microsoft.com")
         or InitiatingProcessFileName =~ "wdavdaemon"
    | project Timestamp, DeviceName, RemoteUrl, RemoteIP, RemotePort,
              InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine
)
| order by Timestamp desc
// CI/CD Linux build host running wdavdaemon OR contacting MDE cloud endpoints.
// Tune the runnerPattern regex to your runner naming convention.
let runnerPattern = @"(?i)(runner|ci|build|gha|github-actions)";
let mdeEndpoints = dynamic(["x.cp.wd.microsoft.com",
                            "global.endpoint.security.microsoft.com",
                            "wdcp.microsoft.com",
                            "cdn.x.cp.wd.microsoft.com"]);
union isfuzzy=true
(   Syslog
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
    | where Computer matches regex runnerPattern
    | where ProcessName =~ "wdavdaemon"
         or SyslogMessage has_any (mdeEndpoints)
         or SyslogMessage has "/opt/microsoft/mdatp"
    | project TimeGenerated, Computer, HostIP, ProcessName, SyslogMessage
),
(   ImNetworkSession
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
    | where DvcHostname matches regex runnerPattern
    | where DstHostname has_any (mdeEndpoints)
         or Url has_any (mdeEndpoints)
    | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, SrcIpAddr, DstIpAddr, DstHostname, DstPortNumber, Url, ActingProcessName
),
(   ImProcessCreate
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
    | where DvcHostname matches regex runnerPattern
    | where TargetProcessName =~ "wdavdaemon"
         or ActingProcessName =~ "wdavdaemon"
    | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, TargetProcessName, TargetProcessCommandLine, ParentProcessName
)
| order by TimeGenerated desc
title: Microsoft Defender Daemon (wdavdaemon) Running on CI/CD Linux Build Host
id: 8f3c2a91-7b4d-4e58-9a12-3e6d8c5f1d04
status: experimental
description: |
  Detects the Microsoft Defender for Endpoint daemon (wdavdaemon) executing on
  Linux hosts whose hostname matches typical CI/CD build runner naming. Mirrors
  the StepSecurity Harden-Runner anomaly that surfaced when GitHub unintentionally
  deployed MDE to hosted Ubuntu runners (July–August 2025, GitHub issue
  actions/runner-images#12656). Build runners should not be running EDR;
  presence indicates infrastructure drift or unauthorised software deployment.
references:
  - https://www.stepsecurity.io/blog/how-stepsecurity-harden-runner-detected-unexpected-microsoft-defender-installation-on-github-hosted-ubuntu-runners
  - https://github.com/actions/runner-images/issues/12656
  - https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/defender-endpoint/microsoft-defender-endpoint-linux
author: SOC Detection Engineering
date: 2026/05/13
tags:
  - attack.t1543.002
  - attack.persistence
logsource:
  category: process_creation
  product: linux
detection:
  selection_proc:
    Image|endswith:
      - '/wdavdaemon'
      - '/mdatp'
  selection_host:
    Hostname|re|i: '(runner|ci|build|gha|github-actions)'
  condition: selection_proc and selection_host
falsepositives:
  - Legitimate MDE rollout to build hosts (requires baseline / allowlist update)
level: medium
(source:linux.auditd OR source:linux.syslog) (@process.name:wdavdaemon OR @process.executable.path:(*/wdavdaemon OR */mdatp) OR @process.command_line:(*x.cp.wd.microsoft.com* OR *global.endpoint.security.microsoft.com* OR *wdcp.microsoft.com*)) @host.name:(*runner* OR *Runner* OR *ci-* OR *CI-* OR *build* OR *Build* OR *gha-* OR *github-actions-*)

Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats summariesonly=t count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime values(Processes.process) as process values(Processes.parent_process_name) as parent_process_name from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes where (Processes.process_name="wdavdaemon" OR Processes.process="*wdavdaemon*") (Processes.dest IN ("*runner*","*Runner*","*ci-*","*CI-*","*build*","*Build*","*gha-*","*github-actions-*")) by Processes.dest Processes.user Processes.process_name | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)` | convert ctime(firstTime) ctime(lastTime) | append [ | tstats summariesonly=t count values(All_Traffic.url) as url values(All_Traffic.dest) as dest from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic where (All_Traffic.dest IN ("x.cp.wd.microsoft.com","global.endpoint.security.microsoft.com","wdcp.microsoft.com","cdn.x.cp.wd.microsoft.com") OR All_Traffic.url="*x.cp.wd.microsoft.com*" OR All_Traffic.url="*global.endpoint.security.microsoft.com*" OR All_Traffic.url="*wdcp.microsoft.com*") (All_Traffic.src IN ("*runner*","*ci-*","*build*","*gha-*","*github-actions-*")) by All_Traffic.src All_Traffic.dest All_Traffic.url | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)` ]
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime values(Processes.process) as process values(Processes.parent_process_name) as parent_process_name from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes where (Processes.process_name="wdavdaemon" OR Processes.process="*wdavdaemon*") (Processes.dest IN ("*runner*","*Runner*","*ci-*","*CI-*","*build*","*Build*","*gha-*","*github-actions-*")) by Processes.dest Processes.user Processes.process_name | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)` | convert ctime(firstTime) ctime(lastTime) | append [ | tstats summariesonly=false count values(All_Traffic.url) as url values(All_Traffic.dest) as dest from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic where (All_Traffic.dest IN ("x.cp.wd.microsoft.com","global.endpoint.security.microsoft.com","wdcp.microsoft.com","cdn.x.cp.wd.microsoft.com") OR All_Traffic.url="*x.cp.wd.microsoft.com*" OR All_Traffic.url="*global.endpoint.security.microsoft.com*" OR All_Traffic.url="*wdcp.microsoft.com*") (All_Traffic.src IN ("*runner*","*ci-*","*build*","*gha-*","*github-actions-*")) by All_Traffic.src All_Traffic.dest All_Traffic.url | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)` ]
[LLM] CI/CD Linux build host outbound to gist.githubusercontent.com (tj-actions IOC pattern) Command & Control High
Hunts for outbound HTTP/HTTPS to gist.githubusercontent.com from CI/CD Linux build hosts, with elevated severity for the exact tj-actions/changed-files (CVE-2025-30066) payload URL containing nikitastupin/30e525b776c409e03c2d6f328f254965 or memdump.py. The article explicitly cites this as the same anomaly class Harden-Runner used to detect the March 2025 supply-chain compromise. Build runners should pull code from registered repos, not random gists. Rationale: Anchors on the exact tj-actions/changed-files (CVE-2025-30066) IOCs corroborated by CISA, Wiz, Snyk, Semgrep and GitHub Advisory Database: malicious payload URL fragment 'nikitastupin/30e525b776c409e03c2d6f328f254965/raw/memdump.py' and malicious commit SHA '0e58ed8671d6b60d0890c21b07f8835ace038e67'. Generic gist.githubusercontent.com hits from CI/CD hosts are alone hunt-tier; the Suspicious_tj_payload flag promotes the exact-match path to high-fidelity alerting. The article explicitly cites this as the same anomaly class that found the original compromise. Cross-checked against: • https://www.cisa.gov/news-events/alerts/2025/03/18/supply-chain-compromise-third-party-tj-actionschanged-files-cve-2025-30066-and-reviewdogaction • https://www.wiz.io/blog/github-action-tj-actions-changed-files-supply-chain-attack-cve-2025-30066 • https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-mrrh-fwg8-r2c3 • https://www.stepsecurity.io/blog/harden-runner-detection-tj-actions-changed-files-action-is-compromised • https://snyk.io/blog/reconstructing-tj-actions-changed-files-github-actions-compromise/
ATT&CKT1195.002 T1567 T1105
Data sourcesWeb.Web Network_Traffic.All_Traffic Endpoint.Processes
// Outbound to gist.githubusercontent.com from CI/CD Linux build hosts — same
// anomaly Harden-Runner used to detect the tj-actions/changed-files compromise
// (CVE-2025-30066, March 2025). The Suspicious_tj column promotes the exact
// malicious payload URL to alerting tier.
let CIDevices = DeviceInfo
    | where Timestamp > ago(1d)
    | where OSPlatform == "Linux"
    | where DeviceName matches regex @"(?i)(runner|ci|build|gha|github-actions)"
         or MachineGroup matches regex @"(?i)(runner|ci|build|github)"
    | summarize make_set(DeviceId);
union isfuzzy=true
(   DeviceNetworkEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
    | where DeviceId in (CIDevices)
    | where RemoteUrl has_any ("gist.githubusercontent.com","gist.github.com")
    | extend Suspicious_tj_payload = RemoteUrl has "nikitastupin/30e525b776c409e03c2d6f328f254965"
                                  or RemoteUrl has "memdump.py"
    | project Timestamp, DeviceName, RemoteUrl, RemoteIP, RemotePort,
              InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
              Suspicious_tj_payload
),
(   DeviceProcessEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
    | where DeviceId in (CIDevices)
    | where FileName in~ ("curl","wget","python","python3","bash","sh")
    | where ProcessCommandLine has_any ("gist.githubusercontent.com","gist.github.com")
    | extend Suspicious_tj_payload = ProcessCommandLine has "nikitastupin/30e525b776c409e03c2d6f328f254965"
                                  or ProcessCommandLine has "memdump.py"
                                  or ProcessCommandLine has "0e58ed8671d6b60d0890c21b07f8835ace038e67"
    | project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine,
              InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
              Suspicious_tj_payload
)
| order by Suspicious_tj_payload desc, Timestamp desc
// Anomalous outbound to gist.githubusercontent.com from CI/CD Linux runners.
// Mirrors the tj-actions/changed-files (CVE-2025-30066) IOC: the malicious
// action fetched a Python memory-dump payload from a public gist.
let runnerPattern = @"(?i)(runner|ci|build|gha|github-actions)";
let tj_actions_payload_marker = dynamic(["nikitastupin/30e525b776c409e03c2d6f328f254965",
                                          "memdump.py",
                                          "0e58ed8671d6b60d0890c21b07f8835ace038e67"]);
union isfuzzy=true
(   ImNetworkSession
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
    | where DvcHostname matches regex runnerPattern
    | where DstHostname has_any ("gist.githubusercontent.com","gist.github.com")
         or Url has_any ("gist.githubusercontent.com","gist.github.com")
    | extend Suspicious_tj_payload = Url has_any (tj_actions_payload_marker)
    | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, SrcIpAddr, DstIpAddr, DstHostname, DstPortNumber, Url,
              ActingProcessName, Suspicious_tj_payload
),
(   ImProcessCreate
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
    | where DvcHostname matches regex runnerPattern
    | where TargetProcessName in~ ("curl","wget","python","python3","bash","sh")
    | where TargetProcessCommandLine has_any ("gist.githubusercontent.com","gist.github.com")
    | extend Suspicious_tj_payload = TargetProcessCommandLine has_any (tj_actions_payload_marker)
    | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, TargetProcessName,
              TargetProcessCommandLine, ParentProcessName, Suspicious_tj_payload
),
(   Syslog
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
    | where Computer matches regex runnerPattern
    | where SyslogMessage has_any ("gist.githubusercontent.com","gist.github.com")
    | extend Suspicious_tj_payload = SyslogMessage has_any (tj_actions_payload_marker)
    | project TimeGenerated, Computer, ProcessName, SyslogMessage, Suspicious_tj_payload
)
| order by Suspicious_tj_payload desc, TimeGenerated desc
title: CI/CD Linux Build Host Outbound to gist.githubusercontent.com (tj-actions IOC)
id: 5d9b4e22-1f7a-43c8-bb15-7c92a4d3e80f
status: experimental
description: |
  Detects outbound HTTP/HTTPS traffic to gist.githubusercontent.com from CI/CD
  Linux build hosts. This is the exact anomaly class StepSecurity Harden-Runner
  used to surface the tj-actions/changed-files supply-chain compromise
  (CVE-2025-30066) in March 2025, where the malicious GitHub Action fetched a
  Python memory-dump payload from a gist. Build runners should clone code from
  registered repositories, not random gists. The exact-payload markers
  (nikitastupin/30e525b776c409e03c2d6f328f254965, memdump.py, commit
  0e58ed8671d6b60d0890c21b07f8835ace038e67) confirm tj-actions exploitation.
references:
  - https://www.stepsecurity.io/blog/how-stepsecurity-harden-runner-detected-unexpected-microsoft-defender-installation-on-github-hosted-ubuntu-runners
  - https://www.stepsecurity.io/blog/harden-runner-detection-tj-actions-changed-files-action-is-compromised
  - https://www.cisa.gov/news-events/alerts/2025/03/18/supply-chain-compromise-third-party-tj-actionschanged-files-cve-2025-30066-and-reviewdogaction
  - https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-mrrh-fwg8-r2c3
author: SOC Detection Engineering
date: 2026/05/13
tags:
  - attack.t1195.002
  - attack.initial_access
  - attack.t1567
  - attack.exfiltration
logsource:
  category: network_connection
  product: linux
detection:
  selection_dest:
    DestinationHostname|contains:
      - 'gist.githubusercontent.com'
      - 'gist.github.com'
  selection_host:
    Hostname|re|i: '(runner|ci|build|gha|github-actions)'
  condition: selection_dest and selection_host
falsepositives:
  - Developer scripts that intentionally bootstrap from a gist (rare; prefer repo-hosted scripts)
level: high
(source:linux.auditd OR source:linux.syslog OR source:kubernetes.audit) (@process.command_line:(*gist.githubusercontent.com* OR *gist.github.com* OR *nikitastupin/30e525b776c409e03c2d6f328f254965* OR *memdump.py* OR *0e58ed8671d6b60d0890c21b07f8835ace038e67*) OR @network.destination.ip:*) @host.name:(*runner* OR *Runner* OR *ci-* OR *CI-* OR *build* OR *Build* OR *gha-* OR *github-actions-*)
WEB
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats summariesonly=t count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime values(Web.url) as url values(Web.dest) as dest values(Web.user) as user values(Web.http_user_agent) as ua from datamodel=Web.Web where (Web.url="*gist.githubusercontent.com*" OR Web.url="*gist.github.com*" OR Web.dest="gist.githubusercontent.com" OR Web.dest="gist.github.com") (Web.src IN ("*runner*","*Runner*","*ci-*","*CI-*","*build*","*Build*","*gha-*","*github-actions-*")) by Web.src Web.dest Web.url | `drop_dm_object_name(Web)` | eval tj_actions_payload=if(match(url,"nikitastupin/30e525b776c409e03c2d6f328f254965") OR match(url,"memdump\.py"),"YES","no") | convert ctime(firstTime) ctime(lastTime) | sort - tj_actions_payload firstTime
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime values(Web.url) as url values(Web.dest) as dest values(Web.user) as user values(Web.http_user_agent) as ua from datamodel=Web.Web where (Web.url="*gist.githubusercontent.com*" OR Web.url="*gist.github.com*" OR Web.dest="gist.githubusercontent.com" OR Web.dest="gist.github.com") (Web.src IN ("*runner*","*Runner*","*ci-*","*CI-*","*build*","*Build*","*gha-*","*github-actions-*")) by Web.src Web.dest Web.url | `drop_dm_object_name(Web)` | eval tj_actions_payload=if(match(url,"nikitastupin/30e525b776c409e03c2d6f328f254965") OR match(url,"memdump\.py"),"YES","no") | convert ctime(firstTime) ctime(lastTime) | sort - tj_actions_payload firstTime
Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children Exploitation Medium
Supply-chain trojan signal: legitimate signed binary spawning script interpreters or exotic LOLBins.
ATT&CKT1195.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceProcessEvents
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine
// Replace `"setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe"` with the vendor binaries observed in the
// article (matching shape with the Defender query).
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where NewProcessName endswith "\powershell.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cmd.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\rundll32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\regsvr32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\mshta.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wmic.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\bitsadmin.exe"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: Trusted Vendor Binary Launching Unusual Children
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d09
status: experimental
description: |
    A signed vendor binary (in the article's named-vendors list)
    spawning script-host or LOLBin children. Strongest signal when
    paired with a recent CVE / supply-chain advisory naming the same
    vendor.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1195/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1195
    - attack.initial-access
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_child:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'
            - '\cmd.exe'
            - '\rundll32.exe'
            - '\regsvr32.exe'
            - '\mshta.exe'
            - '\wscript.exe'
            - '\cscript.exe'
            - '\wmic.exe'
            - '\bitsadmin.exe'
    selection_parent_vendor:
        ParentImage|contains:
            - '\Program Files\'
            - '\Program Files (x86)\'
    condition: selection_child and selection_parent_vendor
falsepositives:
    - Many — installers and updaters legitimately spawn children. Pin
      the parent path to specific vendor binaries observed in the
      article before alerting.
level: medium
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
      AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.parent_process_name, Processes.process_name, Processes.process
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
      AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.parent_process_name, Processes.process_name, Processes.process
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
Critical

CISA KEV: CVE-2025-38352 — Linux Kernel Time-of-Check Time-of-Use (TOCTOU) Race Condition Vulnerability

CISA KEV
2025-09-04 1 use case 0 techniques 2 kill-chain phases detected

Linux kernel contains a time-of-check time-of-use (TOCTOU) race condition vulnerability that has a high impact on confidentiality, integrity, and availability. Vendor: Linux, Product: Kernel. Federal patch due: 2025-09-25.

Actions on Objectives
—
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration. → Asset exposure — vulnerability matches article CVE(s)
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad).
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code.
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key.
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking.
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage.
Asset exposure — vulnerability matches article CVE(s) Reconnaissance High
Match vulnerability scanner output to the CVE(s) named in the article.
ATT&CKT1190
Data sourcesVulnerabilities DeviceInfo DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
// TVM tables are daily snapshots — pin a 1-day window so the engine
// partitions on Timestamp instead of scanning the full retention.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2025-38352")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where Timestamp > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate

// Pre-filter both ASIM tables before the union — same pattern as the
// Defender version so the engine can push hash predicates per-side.
let BadHashes = dynamic(["a566cceaf9a66332470a978a234a8a8e2bbdd4d6aa43c2c75c25a80b3b744307", "b3f83721f24f7ee5eb19f24747b7668ff96da7dfd9be947e6e24a688ecc0a52b", "61f897ed69646e0509f6802fb2d7c5e88c3e3b93c4ca86942e24d203aa878863", "223b873c50380fe9a39f1a22b6abf8d46db506e1c08d08312902f6f3cd1f7ac3", "117305c6c8222162d7246f842c4bb014", "a39696e95a34a017be1435db7ff139d5", "f410d88429b93786b224e489c960bf5c", "be7e2c6a9a4654b51a16f8b10a2be175", "62483e732553c8ba051b792949f3c6d0", "63d22ae0568b760b5e3aabb915313e44"]);
union
    ( ImFileEvent
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where TargetFileSHA256 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileSHA1 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileMD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType,
                  TargetFileName, TargetFilePath, TargetFileSHA256,
                  Cmd = ActingProcessName, Source = "ImFileEvent" ),
    ( ImProcessCreate
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ (BadHashes) or SHA1 in~ (BadHashes) or MD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileName = TargetProcessName, TargetFilePath = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileSHA256 = SHA256,
                  Cmd = TargetProcessCommandLine, Source = "ImProcessCreate" )
| order by TimeGenerated desc

CISA KEV: CVE-2020-24363 — TP-link TL-WA855RE Missing Authentication for Critical Function Vulnerability

CISA KEV
2025-09-02 1 use case 0 techniques 2 kill-chain phases detected

TP-link TL-WA855RE contains a missing authentication for critical function vulnerability. This vulnerability could allow an unauthenticated attacker (on the same network) to submit a TDDP_RESET POST request for a factory reset and reboot. The attacker can then obtain incorrect access control by setting a new administrative password. The impacted products could be end-of-life (EoL) and/or end-of-service (EoS). Users should discontinue product utilization. Vendor: TP-Link, Product: TL-WA855RE. Federal patch due: 2025-09-23.

Suspicious Tag Movement in AWS’s GitHub Action: What Happened and Why It Matters

StepSecurity
2025-08-15 5 use cases 2 techniques 6 kill-chain phases detected

How an AWS release rollback triggered the same red flags as a supply chain attack — and why treating every tag movement as suspicious is key to protecting your CI/CD pipelines

CVEsCVE-2025-30066 CVE-2025-30154
ATT&CKT1190 T1195.002
SHA10e58ed8671d6b60d… f0d342d24037bb11…

[LLM] Tag deletion/repointing on critical GitHub Action repositories (configure-aws-credentials v4.3.0 pattern) Weaponization High
Hunts for `git.delete` or `git.ref.delete` events targeting semantic version tags on watched action repositories — the exact signal that flagged the aws-actions/configure-aws-credentials v4.3.0 incident. Version-tag deletions are vanishingly rare in healthy CI/CD and are the leading indicator of the tj-actions/changed-files and reviewdog/action-setup tag-tampering pattern. Rationale: Article-specific because we target the exact repo (aws-actions/configure-aws-credentials) and the tj-actions/reviewdog repos the article calls out as historical proof-cases. The signal — semantic-version tag deletion on a popular Action — is what StepSecurity's Artifact Monitor flagged on v4.3.0 at ~18:34 UTC and what tj-actions used to compromise pipelines. Defender XDR Advanced Hunting has no native GitHub audit log table, so defender_kql is intentionally empty — port via Sentinel if the GitHub connector is shared. Image refs not fetched because the article images are workflow-run screenshots, not new IOC strings beyond what's already in the text. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/aws-actions/configure-aws-credentials/issues/1419 • https://www.stepsecurity.io/blog/harden-runner-detection-tj-actions-changed-files-action-is-compromised • https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2025-30066
ATT&CKT1195.002 T1554 T1199
Data sourcesChange.All_Changes
// GitHub audit logs land in a custom table via the Microsoft Sentinel GitHub solution.
// Adjust the table name if your tenant uses GitHub_CL or a different connector schema.
let WatchedRepos = dynamic(["aws-actions/configure-aws-credentials","tj-actions/changed-files","reviewdog/action-setup"]);
GitHubAuditLogPolling_CL
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where action_s in ("git.delete","repo.ref.delete","git.ref_delete")
   or (action_s == "git.push" and isnotempty(deleted_b) and deleted_b == true)
| where repo_s in (WatchedRepos) or repo_s startswith "actions/"
| extend RefName = coalesce(ref_s, tostring(parse_json(tostring(data_s)).ref))
| where RefName matches regex @"^refs/tags/v?\d+(\.\d+){0,2}$"
| extend IsAwsV430 = (repo_s == "aws-actions/configure-aws-credentials" and RefName endswith "v4.3.0")
| project TimeGenerated, Actor=actor_s, Repo=repo_s, Action=action_s, Ref=RefName, IsAwsV430, ActorIP=actor_ip_s
| order by TimeGenerated desc
title: GitHub Semantic Version Tag Deletion on Watched Action Repository
id: 9a1f3b2c-4e6d-4f1a-b8a2-7d9e3c1f5b88
description: Detects deletion of a semantic version tag (vX, vX.Y, vX.Y.Z) on critical GitHub Action repositories. Tag deletions are extremely rare in healthy release workflows and were the leading indicator of the tj-actions/changed-files (CVE-2025-30066) and reviewdog/action-setup compromises in March 2025, as well as the AWS configure-aws-credentials v4.3.0 incident on 4 Aug 2025.
references:
  - https://www.stepsecurity.io/blog/suspicious-tag-movement-in-aws-github-action
  - https://github.com/aws-actions/configure-aws-credentials/issues/1419
author: SOC Detection Engineering
date: 2025/08/14
tags:
  - attack.initial_access
  - attack.t1195.002
  - attack.persistence
  - attack.t1554
logsource:
  product: github
  service: audit
detection:
  selection_action:
    action:
      - 'git.delete'
      - 'repo.ref.delete'
      - 'git.ref_delete'
  selection_ref:
    ref|re: '^refs/tags/v?\d+(\.\d+){0,2}$'
  selection_repo:
    repo:
      - 'aws-actions/configure-aws-credentials'
      - 'tj-actions/changed-files'
      - 'reviewdog/action-setup'
  condition: selection_action and selection_ref and selection_repo
falsepositives:
  - Maintainer-initiated hot-fix tag recreation (e.g. the AWS v4.3.0 Release-As re-publish) — must be reconciled against published release notes before closing
level: high
source:github (@action:git.delete OR @action:repo.ref.delete OR @action:git.ref_delete) @repo:(aws-actions/configure-aws-credentials OR tj-actions/changed-files OR reviewdog/action-setup) @ref:(refs/tags/v* OR refs/tags/V*)
CLOUD
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
`summariesonly` | tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime values(Change.user) as actor values(Change.src) as src_ip values(Change.object) as ref from datamodel=Change where Change.vendor_product="GitHub" (Change.action="deleted" OR Change.action="delete") (Change.object_category="tag" OR Change.object="refs/tags/*") Change.object_path IN ("aws-actions/configure-aws-credentials","tj-actions/*","reviewdog/*","actions/*") by Change.object_path Change.object Change.action | `drop_dm_object_name(Change)` | rex field=object "refs/tags/(?<tag_name>v?\d+(\.\d+){0,2})$" | where isnotnull(tag_name) | eval _is_buggy_aws_v430=if(object_path="aws-actions/configure-aws-credentials" AND tag_name="v4.3.0", 1, 0)
summariesonly=false | tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime values(Change.user) as actor values(Change.src) as src_ip values(Change.object) as ref from datamodel=Change where Change.vendor_product="GitHub" (Change.action="deleted" OR Change.action="delete") (Change.object_category="tag" OR Change.object="refs/tags/*") Change.object_path IN ("aws-actions/configure-aws-credentials","tj-actions/*","reviewdog/*","actions/*") by Change.object_path Change.object Change.action | `drop_dm_object_name(Change)` | rex field=object "refs/tags/(?<tag_name>v?\d+(\.\d+){0,2})$" | where isnotnull(tag_name) | eval _is_buggy_aws_v430=if(object_path="aws-actions/configure-aws-credentials" AND tag_name="v4.3.0", 1, 0)
[LLM] Internal workflows pulling aws-actions/configure-aws-credentials@v4.3.0 during the buggy-release window Delivery High
Hunts your org's GitHub Actions workflow runs that resolved `aws-actions/configure-aws-credentials@v4.3.0` (or pinned commit `59b44184`) between 17:49 UTC and 20:47 UTC on 4 Aug 2025 — the precise window when v4.3.0 pointed at the broken commit before being retagged. While this incident proved benign, the same query template is the post-incident sweep you'd run if a future v4.3.0-style tag movement is confirmed malicious. Rationale: Article-specific via two exact strings from the timeline: the action coordinate `aws-actions/configure-aws-credentials@v4.3.0` and the buggy commit SHA `59b44184` (the original v4.3.0 release commit before retag to `d0834ad` at 20:47:54Z). The 17:49–20:47 UTC window is the article's published timeline. Sigma omitted because the detection is a time-bounded cross-table join (workflow_run audit × workflow YAML content), not a single-event shape. Defender XDR has no GitHub workflow_run telemetry, hence empty. Bleeping Computer and GitGuardian writeups of the tj-actions compromise (cited below) validate that consumer-side sweeps keyed on the at-version coordinate plus commit SHA are the post-incident remediation pattern. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/aws-actions/configure-aws-credentials/releases/tag/v4.3.0 • https://github.com/aws-actions/configure-aws-credentials/issues/1419 • https://www.bleepingcomputer.com/news/security/supply-chain-attack-on-popular-github-action-exposes-ci-cd-secrets/
ATT&CKT1195.002 T1078 T1552.004
Data sourcesChange.All_Changes Web.Web
// Sweeps GitHub workflow_run audit events in the buggy-v4.3.0 window. Adapt repo-content
// match (uses_s) to your code-search ingestion table — many orgs ship workflow YAMLs via a
// custom log table populated by `gh api search/code`.
let WindowStart = datetime(2025-08-04T17:49:00Z);
let WindowEnd   = datetime(2025-08-04T20:47:54Z);
let BuggyCommit = "59b44184";
GitHubAuditLogPolling_CL
| where TimeGenerated between (WindowStart .. WindowEnd)
| where action_s in ("workflows.completed_workflow_run","workflow_run","workflows.created_workflow_run")
| extend Repo = repo_s, Actor = actor_s, RunId = tostring(workflow_run_id_d)
| join kind=inner (
    GitHubWorkflowContent_CL
    | where uses_s has "aws-actions/configure-aws-credentials"
    | where uses_s endswith "@v4.3.0" or uses_s contains BuggyCommit
    | project Repo = repo_s, WorkflowFile = path_s, Uses = uses_s
  ) on Repo
| project TimeGenerated, Repo, Actor, WorkflowFile, Uses, RunId
| order by TimeGenerated asc
source:github @action:(workflows.completed_workflow_run OR workflow_run OR workflows.created_workflow_run) (@workflow.uses:*aws-actions/configure-aws-credentials@v4.3.0* OR @workflow.uses:*aws-actions/configure-aws-credentials@59b44184*)
APPLICATION
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
`summariesonly` | tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime values(Change.user) as triggered_by values(Change.object_path) as workflow_repo from datamodel=Change where Change.vendor_product="GitHub" Change.action="workflow_run" earliest="08/04/2025:17:49:00" latest="08/04/2025:20:47:00" by Change.object_path Change.object | `drop_dm_object_name(Change)` | join type=left object_path [ search index=github_workflows ("aws-actions/configure-aws-credentials@v4.3.0" OR "aws-actions/configure-aws-credentials@59b44184") | stats values(uses) as pinned_uses by repo | rename repo as object_path ] | where isnotnull(pinned_uses)
summariesonly=false | tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime values(Change.user) as triggered_by values(Change.object_path) as workflow_repo from datamodel=Change where Change.vendor_product="GitHub" Change.action="workflow_run" earliest="08/04/2025:17:49:00" latest="08/04/2025:20:47:00" by Change.object_path Change.object | `drop_dm_object_name(Change)` | join type=left object_path [ search index=github_workflows ("aws-actions/configure-aws-credentials@v4.3.0" OR "aws-actions/configure-aws-credentials@59b44184") | stats values(uses) as pinned_uses by repo | rename repo as object_path ] | where isnotnull(pinned_uses)
Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children Exploitation Medium
Supply-chain trojan signal: legitimate signed binary spawning script interpreters or exotic LOLBins.
ATT&CKT1195.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceProcessEvents
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine
// Replace `"setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe"` with the vendor binaries observed in the
// article (matching shape with the Defender query).
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where NewProcessName endswith "\powershell.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cmd.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\rundll32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\regsvr32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\mshta.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wmic.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\bitsadmin.exe"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine
| order by TimeGenerated desc

File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match Installation High
Match SHA256/SHA1/MD5 named in the article against EDR file/process telemetry.
ATT&CKT1027
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem Endpoint.Processes DeviceFileEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Pre-filter each table BEFORE the union — the engine cannot push
// predicates across `union`, and DeviceFileEvents lacks ProcessCommandLine
// (Initiating only) so column-projection has to differ per side.
union
    ( DeviceFileEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("0e58ed8671d6b60d0890c21b07f8835ace038e67", "f0d342d24037bb11d26b9bd8496e0808ba32e9ec") or SHA1 in~ ("0e58ed8671d6b60d0890c21b07f8835ace038e67", "f0d342d24037bb11d26b9bd8496e0808ba32e9ec") or MD5 in~ ("0e58ed8671d6b60d0890c21b07f8835ace038e67", "f0d342d24037bb11d26b9bd8496e0808ba32e9ec")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceFileEvents" ),
    ( DeviceProcessEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where AccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("0e58ed8671d6b60d0890c21b07f8835ace038e67", "f0d342d24037bb11d26b9bd8496e0808ba32e9ec") or SHA1 in~ ("0e58ed8671d6b60d0890c21b07f8835ace038e67", "f0d342d24037bb11d26b9bd8496e0808ba32e9ec") or MD5 in~ ("0e58ed8671d6b60d0890c21b07f8835ace038e67", "f0d342d24037bb11d26b9bd8496e0808ba32e9ec")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = ProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceProcessEvents" )
| order by Timestamp desc

// Pre-filter both ASIM tables before the union — same pattern as the
// Defender version so the engine can push hash predicates per-side.
let BadHashes = dynamic(["0e58ed8671d6b60d0890c21b07f8835ace038e67", "f0d342d24037bb11d26b9bd8496e0808ba32e9ec"]);
union
    ( ImFileEvent
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where TargetFileSHA256 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileSHA1 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileMD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType,
                  TargetFileName, TargetFilePath, TargetFileSHA256,
                  Cmd = ActingProcessName, Source = "ImFileEvent" ),
    ( ImProcessCreate
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ (BadHashes) or SHA1 in~ (BadHashes) or MD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileName = TargetProcessName, TargetFilePath = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileSHA256 = SHA256,
                  Cmd = TargetProcessCommandLine, Source = "ImProcessCreate" )
| order by TimeGenerated desc

Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration. → Asset exposure — vulnerability matches article CVE(s)
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad). → [LLM] Compromised tj-actions/changed-files commit SHA referenced on host (CVE-2025-30066 IOC hunt)
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children · Article-specific behavioural hunt — When 'Changed Files' Changed Everything: Our Black Hat 2025 Presentation on the
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key. → File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking. → [LLM] CI/CD runner outbound to gist.githubusercontent.com (tj-actions CVE-2025-30066 staging fetch)
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage. → [LLM] Runner.Worker process memory dump via memdump.py on CI/CD runner (tj-actions credential theft)
[LLM] CI/CD runner outbound to gist.githubusercontent.com (tj-actions CVE-2025-30066 staging fetch) Command & Control High
Detects the stage-2 payload retrieval performed by the compromised tj-actions/changed-files action: a GitHub Actions runner process fetching memdump.py from a public GitHub Gist. This is the exact signal StepSecurity's Harden Runner used to catch the breach — a never-before-seen destination in the baselined runner network profile, leveraging a high-reputation GitHub-owned domain that EDR allowlists implicitly trust. Rationale: The article explicitly names gist.githubusercontent.com as the staging URL the compromised action contacted to fetch memdump.py — and identifies this anomalous egress as the single signal that exposed the attack against a GitHub-owned, high-reputation domain. Filtering on runner-context processes (Runner.Worker, node, curl, python) removes the noise of developer workstations grabbing gists. CVE-2025-30066 is the assigned identifier. Cross-checked against: • https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2025-30066 • https://www.cisa.gov/news-events/alerts/2025/03/18/supply-chain-compromise-third-party-github-action-cve-2025-30066 • https://www.wiz.io/blog/github-action-tj-actions-changed-files-supply-chain-attack-cve-2025-30066 • https://github.com/tj-actions/changed-files/issues/2463
ATT&CKT1195.002 T1105 T1567

   or InitiatingProcessParentFileName in~ ("Runner.Worker.exe","Runner.Worker","Runner.Listener.exe","Runner.Listener","node.exe","node")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, RemoteUrl, RemoteIP, RemotePort,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessParentFileName,
          InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFolderPath, ReportId
| order by Timestamp desc

[LLM] Runner.Worker process memory dump via memdump.py on CI/CD runner (tj-actions credential theft) Actions on Objectives High
Detects the credential-access stage of the tj-actions compromise: a Python interpreter on a GitHub Actions runner reading /proc/<Runner.Worker_PID>/mem or executing memdump.py to scrape CI/CD secrets from the Runner.Worker process address space. This is the specific TTP that turned the supply-chain foothold into thousands of leaked AWS keys and GitHub tokens. Rationale: The article explicitly names memdump.py as the payload and Runner.Worker as the target process whose memory was dumped to extract CI/CD secrets. Anchoring on a Python interpreter executing 'memdump.py' or reading /proc/<pid>/mem of Runner.Worker is article-specific and unique to this attack chain — no legitimate CI workflow needs to read another process's memory. Cross-checked against: • https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2025-30066 • https://www.cisa.gov/news-events/alerts/2025/03/18/supply-chain-compromise-third-party-github-action-cve-2025-30066 • https://www.wiz.io/blog/github-action-tj-actions-changed-files-supply-chain-attack-cve-2025-30066 • https://unit42.paloaltonetworks.com/github-actions-supply-chain-attack/
ATT&CKT1003.007 T1212 T1552.001

         and (ProcessCommandLine has "memdump.py"
              or ProcessCommandLine has "Runner.Worker"
              or ProcessCommandLine matches regex @"(?i)/proc/\d+/mem"
              or ProcessCommandLine has "gist.githubusercontent.com"))
   or ProcessCommandLine has "memdump.py"

   or DeviceName has_any ("runner","gh-actions","github-actions","ci-","build")

          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessParentFileName,

[LLM] Compromised tj-actions/changed-files commit SHA referenced on host (CVE-2025-30066 IOC hunt) Delivery High
Hunts for any process command line, file write, or workflow invocation that references the published malicious commit SHAs of tj-actions/changed-files. Triggers when a runner performs `git checkout <bad-sha>`, when a workflow.yml on disk pins the action to the malicious SHA, or when build logs containing the SHA are written. This is the cleanest IOC pivot — these eight SHAs are post-mortem confirmed and unique to the breach. Rationale: The pre-extracted IOC list contains eight SHA1 commit hashes corroborated by CISA's advisory and the public tj-actions GitHub Security Advisory as the malicious commits inserted via imposter-commit/tag-rewrite. Any appearance of these SHAs in a process command line, file content, or workflow.yml on disk is high-fidelity evidence the runner consumed the compromised action — full-string match keeps false-positive rate near-zero. Cross-checked against: • https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2025-30066 • https://www.cisa.gov/news-events/alerts/2025/03/18/supply-chain-compromise-third-party-github-action-cve-2025-30066 • https://github.com/tj-actions/changed-files/issues/2463 • https://www.wiz.io/blog/github-action-tj-actions-changed-files-supply-chain-attack-cve-2025-30066 • https://unit42.paloaltonetworks.com/github-actions-supply-chain-attack/
ATT&CKT1195.002 T1505.003 T1059.006

union isfuzzy=true
(
    DeviceProcessEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(90d)
    | where ProcessCommandLine has_any (MaliciousSHAs) or InitiatingProcessCommandLine has_any (MaliciousSHAs)
    | project Timestamp, EventType="Process", DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, ReportId
),
(
    DeviceFileEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(90d)
    | where FolderPath has_any (".github/workflows","action.yml","action.yaml",".gitlab-ci.yml")

    | where InitiatingProcessCommandLine has_any (MaliciousSHAs)
    | project Timestamp, EventType="File", DeviceName, FileName, FolderPath, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, SHA256, ReportId
)
| order by Timestamp desc
let MaliciousSHAs = dynamic(["0e58ed8671d6b60d0890c21b07f8835ace038e67","6e6023c01918b353229af0881232f601a4cc8365","fbc2c5ebe64389f297a7808025379f77133f1292","e1e36574b3af1ddaab74f5e69505d8836bf12f52","ce4a123414f9fffa959d1f329c4749da83c4bf10","c17ac4b5c1cb901a7ccddf00ac9722b8e2725345","3f401fe1d58fe77e10d665ab713057375e39b887","f5434e31b6259b4e08684618a305bae127b6d784"]);

| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

// Sentinel has no native TVM table. If MDE→Sentinel streaming is enabled,
// `DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities` is available. Otherwise this UC has
// no Sentinel translation — track via Update / Heartbeat OS-version
// gaps as a coarse approximation.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2025-30066", "CVE-2025-30154")

Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children Exploitation Medium
Supply-chain trojan signal: legitimate signed binary spawning script interpreters or exotic LOLBins.
ATT&CKT1195.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceProcessEvents
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine
// Replace `"setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe"` with the vendor binaries observed in the
// article (matching shape with the Defender query).
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where NewProcessName endswith "\powershell.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cmd.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\rundll32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\regsvr32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\mshta.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wmic.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\bitsadmin.exe"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine
| order by TimeGenerated desc

// Pre-filter both ASIM tables before the union — same pattern as the
// Defender version so the engine can push hash predicates per-side.
let BadHashes = dynamic(["0e58ed8671d6b60d0890c21b07f8835ace038e67", "6e6023c01918b353229af0881232f601a4cc8365", "fbc2c5ebe64389f297a7808025379f77133f1292", "e1e36574b3af1ddaab74f5e69505d8836bf12f52", "ce4a123414f9fffa959d1f329c4749da83c4bf10", "c17ac4b5c1cb901a7ccddf00ac9722b8e2725345", "3f401fe1d58fe77e10d665ab713057375e39b887", "f5434e31b6259b4e08684618a305bae127b6d784"]);
union
    ( ImFileEvent
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where TargetFileSHA256 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileSHA1 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileMD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType,
                  TargetFileName, TargetFilePath, TargetFileSHA256,
                  Cmd = ActingProcessName, Source = "ImFileEvent" ),
    ( ImProcessCreate
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ (BadHashes) or SHA1 in~ (BadHashes) or MD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileName = TargetProcessName, TargetFilePath = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileSHA256 = SHA256,

        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"

           or TargetFileSHA1 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileMD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType,
                  TargetFileName, TargetFilePath, TargetFileSHA256,
                  Cmd = ActingProcessName, Source = "ImFileEvent" ),

        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"

                  TargetFileSHA256 = SHA256,
                  Cmd = TargetProcessCommandLine, Source = "ImProcessCreate" )
| order by TimeGenerated desc

id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d0b
status: experimental
description: |
    Replace `__HASH_LIST__` placeholder with article-specific SHA256
    values. Use a watchlist or external indicator file for management
    at scale.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1027/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1027
    - attack.defense-evasion
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection:
        Hashes|contains:
            - 'SHA256=__HASH_PLACEHOLDER_REPLACE_ME__'
    condition: selection
falsepositives:
    - None on a true-positive hash match
level: critical
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.

CISA KEV: CVE-2007-0671 — Microsoft Office Excel Remote Code Execution Vulnerability

CISA KEV
2025-08-12 1 use case 1 technique 3 kill-chain phases detected

Microsoft Office Excel contains a remote code execution vulnerability that can be exploited when a specially crafted Excel file is opened. This malicious file could be delivered as an email attachment or hosted on a malicious website. An attacker could leverage this vulnerability by creating a specially crafted Excel file, which, when opened, allowing an attacker to execute remote code on the affected system. Vendor: Microsoft, Product: Office. Federal patch due: 2025-09-02.

CVEsCVE-2007-0671
ATT&CKT1190

Actions on Objectives
—
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration. → Asset exposure — vulnerability matches article CVE(s)
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad).
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code.
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key.
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking.
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage.
Asset exposure — vulnerability matches article CVE(s) Reconnaissance High
Match vulnerability scanner output to the CVE(s) named in the article.
ATT&CKT1190
Data sourcesVulnerabilities DeviceInfo DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
// TVM tables are daily snapshots — pin a 1-day window so the engine
// partitions on Timestamp instead of scanning the full retention.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2007-0671")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where Timestamp > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

// Sentinel has no native TVM table. If MDE→Sentinel streaming is enabled,
// `DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities` is available. Otherwise this UC has
// no Sentinel translation — track via Update / Heartbeat OS-version
// gaps as a coarse approximation.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2007-0671")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId,
          VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc
APPLICATION
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.

    from datamodel=Vulnerabilities
    where Vulnerabilities.signature IN ("CVE-2007-0671")
    by Vulnerabilities.dest, Vulnerabilities.signature, Vulnerabilities.severity, Vulnerabilities.cve
| `drop_dm_object_name(Vulnerabilities)`
| sort - severity
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Vulnerabilities

    by Vulnerabilities.dest, Vulnerabilities.signature, Vulnerabilities.severity, Vulnerabilities.cve
| `drop_dm_object_name(Vulnerabilities)`

CISA KEV: CVE-2013-3893 — Microsoft Internet Explorer Resource Management Errors Vulnerability

CISA KEV
2025-08-12 3 use cases 1 technique 4 kill-chain phases detected

Microsoft Internet Explorer contains a memory corruption vulnerability that allows for remote code execution. The impacted products could be end-of-life (EoL) and/or end-of-service (EoS). Users should discontinue product utilization. Vendor: Microsoft, Product: Internet Explorer. Federal patch due: 2025-09-02.

// Pre-filter each table BEFORE the union — the engine cannot push

                  Cmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceFileEvents" ),
    ( DeviceProcessEvents

// Pre-filter both ASIM tables before the union — same pattern as the
// Defender version so the engine can push hash predicates per-side.
let BadHashes = dynamic(["58dc05118ef8b11dcb5f5c596ab772fd", "4d257e569539973ab0bbafee8fb87582", "dbdb1032d7bb4757d6011fb1d077856c", "645e29b7c6319295ae8b13ce8575dc1d", "e9c73997694a897d3c6aadb26ed34797", "1b03e3de1ef3e7135fbf9d5ce7e7ccf6", "bf891c72e4c29cfbe533756ea5685314"]);
union
    ( ImFileEvent
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where TargetFileSHA256 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileSHA1 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileMD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType,
                  TargetFileName, TargetFilePath, TargetFileSHA256,
                  Cmd = ActingProcessName, Source = "ImFileEvent" ),
    ( ImProcessCreate
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ (BadHashes) or SHA1 in~ (BadHashes) or MD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileName = TargetProcessName, TargetFilePath = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileSHA256 = SHA256,
                  Cmd = TargetProcessCommandLine, Source = "ImProcessCreate" )
| order by TimeGenerated desc

Lessons from AWS CodeBuild’s Memory-Dump Incident (CVE-2025-8217)

StepSecurity
2025-08-09 6 use cases 3 techniques 6 kill-chain phases detected

How threat actors exploited AWS CodeBuild pipelines by stealing secrets from CI/CD memory—and the proactive defenses organizations can deploy to detect, respond to, and prevent such attacks.

CVEsCVE-2025-8217 CVE-2025-30066 CVE-2025-30154

Actions on Objectives
Detected
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration. → Asset exposure — vulnerability matches article CVE(s)
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad).
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → PowerShell encoded / obfuscated command · Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key. → RMM tool installed by non-IT user — remote-access utility for hands-on-keyboard · File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking.
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage. → [LLM] Linux process opens /proc/<pid>/mem or /proc/<pid>/maps on a build/CI host (CVE-2025-8217 / CVE-2025-30066 memory dump TTP)
[LLM] Linux process opens /proc/<pid>/mem or /proc/<pid>/maps on a build/CI host (CVE-2025-8217 / CVE-2025-30066 memory dump TTP) Actions on Objectives High
Hunts the literal credential-dump TTP shown in the article's Python listing: a non-debugger Linux process reading another process's address space via /proc/<N>/mem and /proc/<N>/maps to scrape repo tokens (e.g. searches /proc/*/cmdline for 'Runner.Worker' before reading its memory). This is the shared core technique behind both the AWS CodeBuild incident (AWS-2025-016, CVE-2025-8217) and the tj-actions/changed-files / reviewdog supply-chain attacks (CVE-2025-30066) — and is specifically what StepSecurity Harden-Runner and Sysdig's 'Dump Memory using /proc/ Filesystem' rule alert on. Rationale: Anchored on the verbatim Python payload reproduced in the article (`open('/proc/{pid}/maps','r')`, `open('/proc/{pid}/mem','rb')`, search-for-'Runner.Worker' in /proc/*/cmdline) — the same code-path StepSecurity simulated in their Harden-Runner demo. Cross-validated against CISA's CVE-2025-30066 advisory, the AWS-2025-016 bulletin (confirming token theft for AWS Toolkit for VS Code + AWS SDK for .NET repos), Wiz's tj-actions write-up and Sysdig's existing 'Dump Memory using /proc/ Filesystem' Falco rule. Reads of /proc/<N>/mem by anything other than a small list of debuggers/crash-handlers is a near-zero-FP signal on a CI build host, where the runner process is the intended secret-bearing target. Cross-checked against: • https://www.cisa.gov/news-events/alerts/2025/03/18/supply-chain-compromise-third-party-tj-actionschanged-files-cve-2025-30066-and-reviewdogaction • https://aws.amazon.com/security/security-bulletins/aws-2025-016/ • https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2025-8217 • https://www.sysdig.com/blog/detecting-and-mitigating-the-tj-actions-changed-files-supply-chain-attack-cve-2025-30066 • https://www.wiz.io/blog/github-action-tj-actions-changed-files-supply-chain-attack-cve-2025-30066 • https://www.sophos.com/en-us/security-advisories/sophos-sa-20250317-tj-action-compromise-0
ATT&CKT1003.007 T1552.001 T1195.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem
// Linux-only — /proc is a Linux concept. Restrict to Linux endpoints (CI runners, build agents).
let _linux_hosts = DeviceInfo
    | where Timestamp > ago(1d)
    | where OSPlatform == "Linux"
    | summarize by DeviceId;
let _debuggers = dynamic(["gdb","lldb","strace","ltrace","perf","systemd-coredump","gcore","valgrind","apport","abrt-hook-ccpp","crash"]);
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where DeviceId in (_linux_hosts)
// Article's Python payload: open('/proc/{pid}/maps','r') and open('/proc/{pid}/mem','rb')
| where FolderPath matches regex @"^/proc/\d+/?$"
| where FileName in~ ("mem","maps")
| where InitiatingProcessFileName !in~ (_debuggers)
// Exclude self-introspection (process reading /proc/self/* — common in libc/golang runtimes)
| extend AccessedPid = extract(@"^/proc/(\d+)", 1, FolderPath)
| where AccessedPid != tostring(InitiatingProcessId)
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFolderPath, InitiatingProcessParentFileName,
          FolderPath, FileName, AccessedPid, InitiatingProcessSHA256
| order by Timestamp desc
// Sentinel ASIM file events — covers auditd / Sysmon-for-Linux / MDE-Linux normalised feeds
let _debuggers = dynamic(["gdb","lldb","strace","ltrace","perf","systemd-coredump","gcore","valgrind","apport"]);
ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventType in~ ("FileAccessed","FileOpened","FileRead","FileCreated")
// Article's payload reads /proc/<N>/mem and /proc/<N>/maps
| where TargetFilePath matches regex @"^/proc/\d+/(mem|maps)$"
| where ActingProcessName !in~ (_debuggers)
| extend AccessedPid = extract(@"^/proc/(\d+)/", 1, TargetFilePath)
| where AccessedPid != tostring(ActingProcessId)
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername,
          ActingProcessName, ActingProcessCommandLine,
          TargetFilePath, AccessedPid, Hash
| order by TimeGenerated desc
title: Linux Memory Dump via /proc/<pid>/mem (CVE-2025-8217 / CVE-2025-30066 TTP)
id: 9a3c4b71-2d8e-4f51-b6a0-1c5e7d8f0a23
description: |
  Detects a non-debugger Linux process opening /proc/<N>/mem or /proc/<N>/maps —
  the exact credential-dump technique used in the AWS CodeBuild July 2025
  incident (CVE-2025-8217) and the tj-actions/changed-files + reviewdog
  supply-chain attacks (CVE-2025-30066). The malicious payload scans
  /proc/*/cmdline for the CI runner process (e.g. 'Runner.Worker') then reads
  its memory regions to extract repository access tokens.
references:
  - https://www.stepsecurity.io/blog/lessons-from-aws-codebuilds-memory-dump-incident-cve-2025-8217
  - https://aws.amazon.com/security/security-bulletins/aws-2025-016/
  - https://www.cisa.gov/news-events/alerts/2025/03/18/supply-chain-compromise-third-party-tj-actionschanged-files-cve-2025-30066-and-reviewdogaction
  - https://www.sysdig.com/blog/detecting-and-mitigating-the-tj-actions-changed-files-supply-chain-attack-cve-2025-30066
  - https://www.wiz.io/blog/github-action-tj-actions-changed-files-supply-chain-attack-cve-2025-30066
author: SOC Detection Engineering
date: 2026/05/13
tags:
  - attack.credential_access
  - attack.t1003.007
  - attack.t1552.001
  - attack.t1195.002
  - cve.2025.8217
  - cve.2025.30066
logsource:
  product: linux
  category: file_access
detection:
  selection_mem:
    TargetFilename|re: '^/proc/\d+/(mem|maps)$'
  filter_debuggers:
    Image|endswith:
      - '/gdb'
      - '/lldb'
      - '/strace'
      - '/ltrace'
      - '/perf'
      - '/systemd-coredump'
      - '/gcore'
      - '/valgrind'
      - '/apport'
  condition: selection_mem and not filter_debuggers
falsepositives:
  - Debuggers, crash reporters, profilers (gdb, strace, perf, systemd-coredump)
  - Container/runtime introspection of /proc/self/* (mitigated by AccessedPid != self filter in KQL/SPL versions)
level: high
source:linux.auditd (@auditd.type:(OPENAT OR OPEN OR PATH)) (@file.path:/proc/*/mem OR @file.path:/proc/*/maps) -@process.name:(gdb OR lldb OR strace OR ltrace OR perf OR systemd-coredump OR gcore OR valgrind OR apport)
ENDPOINT

Asset exposure — vulnerability matches article CVE(s) Reconnaissance High
Match vulnerability scanner output to the CVE(s) named in the article.
ATT&CKT1190
Data sourcesVulnerabilities DeviceInfo DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
// TVM tables are daily snapshots — pin a 1-day window so the engine
// partitions on Timestamp instead of scanning the full retention.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2025-8217", "CVE-2025-30066", "CVE-2025-30154")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where Timestamp > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

// Sentinel has no native TVM table. If MDE→Sentinel streaming is enabled,
// `DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities` is available. Otherwise this UC has
// no Sentinel translation — track via Update / Heartbeat OS-version
// gaps as a coarse approximation.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2025-8217", "CVE-2025-30066", "CVE-2025-30154")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId,
          VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

PowerShell encoded / obfuscated command Exploitation High
Encoded or obfuscated PowerShell — common across loaders, recon, and post-exploitation.
ATT&CKT1059.001 T1027
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceProcessEvents
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe")
| where ProcessCommandLine matches regex @"(?i)(-enc|encodedcommand|frombase64string|-nop|-w\s+hidden|invoke-expression|iex\s*\(|downloadstring|net\.webclient)"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, ProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where NewProcessName endswith "\powershell.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\pwsh.exe"
| where CommandLine matches regex @"(?i)(-enc|encodedcommand|frombase64string|-nop|-w\s+hidden|invoke-expression|iex\s*\(|downloadstring|net\.webclient)"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, NewProcessName,
          CommandLine, ParentProcessName
| order by TimeGenerated desc

description: |

    .WebClient.DownloadString — combinations indicating evasion.

    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1027/

date: 2026/05/02

File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match Installation High
Match SHA256/SHA1/MD5 named in the article against EDR file/process telemetry.
ATT&CKT1027
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem Endpoint.Processes DeviceFileEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Pre-filter each table BEFORE the union — the engine cannot push
// predicates across `union`, and DeviceFileEvents lacks ProcessCommandLine
// (Initiating only) so column-projection has to differ per side.
union
    ( DeviceFileEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("0e58ed8671d6b60d0890c21b07f8835ace038e67") or SHA1 in~ ("0e58ed8671d6b60d0890c21b07f8835ace038e67") or MD5 in~ ("0e58ed8671d6b60d0890c21b07f8835ace038e67")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceFileEvents" ),
    ( DeviceProcessEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where AccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("0e58ed8671d6b60d0890c21b07f8835ace038e67") or SHA1 in~ ("0e58ed8671d6b60d0890c21b07f8835ace038e67") or MD5 in~ ("0e58ed8671d6b60d0890c21b07f8835ace038e67")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = ProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceProcessEvents" )
| order by Timestamp desc

// Pre-filter both ASIM tables before the union — same pattern as the
// Defender version so the engine can push hash predicates per-side.
let BadHashes = dynamic(["0e58ed8671d6b60d0890c21b07f8835ace038e67"]);
union
    ( ImFileEvent
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where TargetFileSHA256 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileSHA1 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileMD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType,
                  TargetFileName, TargetFilePath, TargetFileSHA256,
                  Cmd = ActingProcessName, Source = "ImFileEvent" ),
    ( ImProcessCreate
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ (BadHashes) or SHA1 in~ (BadHashes) or MD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileName = TargetProcessName, TargetFilePath = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileSHA256 = SHA256,
                  Cmd = TargetProcessCommandLine, Source = "ImProcessCreate" )
| order by TimeGenerated desc

Actions on Objectives
Likely
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration.
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad). → [LLM] PyPI install footprint of num2words v0.5.15/0.5.16 (Scavenger supply-chain compromise)
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key. → [LLM] Scavenger loader/stealer SHA256 execution or drop on endpoint · File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking. → [LLM] Scavenger C2 callback: ifyouseethisyouareultragay[.]com / pokerainteasy[.]su · Network connections to article IPs / domains
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage.
[LLM] PyPI install footprint of num2words v0.5.15/0.5.16 (Scavenger supply-chain compromise) Delivery High
Detects developer/CI hosts that pulled the Scavenger-poisoned num2words releases. Hunts for pip/uv/poetry installing the exact compromised versions, and for the site-packages directory/_build.py loader DLL dropped by the malicious __init__.py. Both 0.5.15 and 0.5.16 were yanked by PyPI; any presence post-July-28 is actionable. Rationale: v0.5.15 and v0.5.16 are exact strings — the article names 0.5.15 as compromised; InvokeRE and OSV/MAL-2025-6794 confirm 0.5.16 was also published before being yanked. The malicious add-on is a single `_build.py` file inside the `num2words/` package directory (per InvokeRE reverse-engineering) — that file in that path is a uniquely high-fidelity drop. No FPs expected since both versions are gone from PyPI. Cross-checked against: • https://invokere.com/posts/2025/07/scavenger-malware-distributed-via-num2words-pypi-supply-chain-compromise/ • https://github.com/Invoke-RE/community-malware-research/blob/main/Research/Loaders/Scavenger/num2words_IOCs.md • https://osv.dev/vulnerability/MAL-2025-6794 • https://security.snyk.io/vuln/SNYK-PYTHON-NUM2WORDS-11172937 • https://blog.pypi.org/posts/2025-07-31-incident-report-phishing-attack/
ATT&CKT1195.002 T1059.006
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes Endpoint.Filesystem
union
(DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where FileName in~ ("pip.exe","pip3.exe","python.exe","python3.exe","uv.exe","poetry.exe")
    or InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("pip.exe","pip3.exe","python.exe","python3.exe","uv.exe","poetry.exe")
| where ProcessCommandLine has_any ("num2words==0.5.15","num2words==0.5.16","num2words-0.5.15","num2words-0.5.16")
    or InitiatingProcessCommandLine has_any ("num2words==0.5.15","num2words==0.5.16")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine),
(DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where (FolderPath has "site-packages" and FolderPath has_any (@"num2words-0.5.15", @"num2words-0.5.16"))
    or (FolderPath has @"\num2words\" and FileName =~ "_build.py" and InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("pip.exe","python.exe","python3.exe","uv.exe"))
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, FolderPath, FileName, SHA256, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine)
| order by Timestamp desc
union isfuzzy=true
(SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where (NewProcessName endswith @"\pip.exe" or NewProcessName endswith @"\pip3.exe" or NewProcessName endswith @"\python.exe" or NewProcessName endswith @"\python3.exe" or NewProcessName endswith @"\uv.exe" or NewProcessName endswith @"\poetry.exe")
| where CommandLine has_any ("num2words==0.5.15","num2words==0.5.16","num2words-0.5.15","num2words-0.5.16")
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, NewProcessName, CommandLine, ParentProcessName),
(ImProcessCreate
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where TargetProcessName has_any ("pip","python","uv","poetry")
| where TargetProcessCommandLine has_any ("num2words==0.5.15","num2words==0.5.16","num2words-0.5.15","num2words-0.5.16")
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, TargetProcessName, TargetProcessCommandLine, ParentProcessName),
(ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where (TargetFilePath has "site-packages" and TargetFilePath has_any ("num2words-0.5.15","num2words-0.5.16"))
    or (TargetFilePath has @"\num2words\" and TargetFileName =~ "_build.py")
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, TargetFilePath, TargetFileName, TargetFileSHA256, ActingProcessName)
| order by TimeGenerated desc

[LLM] Scavenger C2 callback: ifyouseethisyouareultragay[.]com / pokerainteasy[.]su Command & Control High
Detects egress to the two confirmed Scavenger loader C2 hosts referenced in InvokeRE's reverse-engineering of the num2words v0.5.15 loader. These hostnames are unique, lexically distinctive strings that should never appear in legitimate traffic — any DNS resolution or HTTP(S) connection is post-install-stage execution. Rationale: Both C2 strings are cross-confirmed in InvokeRE's reversing writeup and the Invoke-RE community IOC repo. Their lexical distinctiveness (one is a slur-laced novelty domain, the other a `.su` ccTLD) means zero collision with benign traffic — `has` match is sufficient. Sigma kept simple (single dns selection block). Cross-checked against: • https://invokere.com/posts/2025/07/scavenger-malware-distributed-via-num2words-pypi-supply-chain-compromise/ • https://github.com/Invoke-RE/community-malware-research/blob/main/Research/Loaders/Scavenger/num2words_IOCs.md
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1568

[LLM] Scavenger loader/stealer SHA256 execution or drop on endpoint Installation High
Hunts for the four confirmed Scavenger payload hashes (one loader + three stealer variants: electric/exodus/shiny) appearing as a file write, image load, or process execution on any endpoint. These hashes were extracted from the num2words v0.5.15 payload chain by InvokeRE. Catches the post-installation stages that drop version.dll/umpdc.dll/profapi.dll side-loading stealers targeting .pypirc, Electrum wallet config, and Exodus wallet binaries. Rationale: The four SHA256s are extracted directly from InvokeRE's reverse-engineering writeup and the Invoke-RE community IOC repository. SHA256 collisions on a hash sweep are essentially impossible, giving a near-zero FP rate. Covers post-install stages the StepSecurity article notes but doesn't enumerate — useful for hunting laterally if the install-stage UC1 has already fired on one host. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/Invoke-RE/community-malware-research/blob/main/Research/Loaders/Scavenger/num2words_IOCs.md • https://invokere.com/posts/2025/07/scavenger-malware-distributed-via-num2words-pypi-supply-chain-compromise/
ATT&CKT1574.002 T1555 T1552.001
