let scavenger_hashes = dynamic([
  "be917cb379b9622f56a4d5ec93bf00c20cb76c6646e5919690d0f7c09c956de2",
  "c2a7ee6ab9344e1bb13c61dc689d4a946678e0505367cd55c9b43ddee3d461e2",
  "439da8bb9c541d26b0f534b17d75790e252e4d9058561e8907f8690e21cd0616",
  "c36ebf96573afcb36bb31590d56e8af49502fb159e00fd4a59336f8a450bec8b"
]);
union
(DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where SHA256 in~ (scavenger_hashes)
| project Timestamp, Source="DeviceFileEvents", DeviceName, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessAccountName),
(DeviceImageLoadEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where SHA256 in~ (scavenger_hashes)
| project Timestamp, Source="DeviceImageLoadEvents", DeviceName, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessAccountName=InitiatingProcessAccountName),
(DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where SHA256 in~ (scavenger_hashes) or InitiatingProcessSHA256 in~ (scavenger_hashes)
| project Timestamp, Source="DeviceProcessEvents", DeviceName, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessAccountName)
| order by Timestamp desc

Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children Exploitation Medium
Supply-chain trojan signal: legitimate signed binary spawning script interpreters or exotic LOLBins.
ATT&CKT1195.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceProcessEvents
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine
// Replace `"setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe"` with the vendor binaries observed in the
// article (matching shape with the Defender query).
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where NewProcessName endswith "\powershell.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cmd.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\rundll32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\regsvr32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\mshta.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wmic.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\bitsadmin.exe"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: Trusted Vendor Binary Launching Unusual Children
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d09
status: experimental
description: |
    A signed vendor binary (in the article's named-vendors list)
    spawning script-host or LOLBin children. Strongest signal when
    paired with a recent CVE / supply-chain advisory naming the same
    vendor.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1195/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1195
    - attack.initial-access
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_child:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'
            - '\cmd.exe'
            - '\rundll32.exe'
            - '\regsvr32.exe'
            - '\mshta.exe'
            - '\wscript.exe'
            - '\cscript.exe'
            - '\wmic.exe'
            - '\bitsadmin.exe'
    selection_parent_vendor:
        ParentImage|contains:
            - '\Program Files\'
            - '\Program Files (x86)\'
    condition: selection_child and selection_parent_vendor
falsepositives:
    - Many — installers and updaters legitimately spawn children. Pin
      the parent path to specific vendor binaries observed in the
      article before alerting.
level: medium
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
      AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.parent_process_name, Processes.process_name, Processes.process
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
      AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.parent_process_name, Processes.process_name, Processes.process
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`

Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem Endpoint.Processes DeviceFileEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Pre-filter each table BEFORE the union — the engine cannot push
// predicates across `union`, and DeviceFileEvents lacks ProcessCommandLine
// (Initiating only) so column-projection has to differ per side.
union
    ( DeviceFileEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("be917cb379b9622f56a4d5ec93bf00c20cb76c6646e5919690d0f7c09c956de2", "c2a7ee6ab9344e1bb13c61dc689d4a946678e0505367cd55c9b43ddee3d461e2", "439da8bb9c541d26b0f534b17d75790e252e4d9058561e8907f8690e21cd0616", "c36ebf96573afcb36bb31590d56e8af49502fb159e00fd4a59336f8a450bec8b") or SHA1 in~ ("be917cb379b9622f56a4d5ec93bf00c20cb76c6646e5919690d0f7c09c956de2", "c2a7ee6ab9344e1bb13c61dc689d4a946678e0505367cd55c9b43ddee3d461e2", "439da8bb9c541d26b0f534b17d75790e252e4d9058561e8907f8690e21cd0616", "c36ebf96573afcb36bb31590d56e8af49502fb159e00fd4a59336f8a450bec8b") or MD5 in~ ("be917cb379b9622f56a4d5ec93bf00c20cb76c6646e5919690d0f7c09c956de2", "c2a7ee6ab9344e1bb13c61dc689d4a946678e0505367cd55c9b43ddee3d461e2", "439da8bb9c541d26b0f534b17d75790e252e4d9058561e8907f8690e21cd0616", "c36ebf96573afcb36bb31590d56e8af49502fb159e00fd4a59336f8a450bec8b")

                  Cmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceFileEvents" ),
    ( DeviceProcessEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where AccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("be917cb379b9622f56a4d5ec93bf00c20cb76c6646e5919690d0f7c09c956de2", "c2a7ee6ab9344e1bb13c61dc689d4a946678e0505367cd55c9b43ddee3d461e2", "439da8bb9c541d26b0f534b17d75790e252e4d9058561e8907f8690e21cd0616", "c36ebf96573afcb36bb31590d56e8af49502fb159e00fd4a59336f8a450bec8b") or SHA1 in~ ("be917cb379b9622f56a4d5ec93bf00c20cb76c6646e5919690d0f7c09c956de2", "c2a7ee6ab9344e1bb13c61dc689d4a946678e0505367cd55c9b43ddee3d461e2", "439da8bb9c541d26b0f534b17d75790e252e4d9058561e8907f8690e21cd0616", "c36ebf96573afcb36bb31590d56e8af49502fb159e00fd4a59336f8a450bec8b") or MD5 in~ ("be917cb379b9622f56a4d5ec93bf00c20cb76c6646e5919690d0f7c09c956de2", "c2a7ee6ab9344e1bb13c61dc689d4a946678e0505367cd55c9b43ddee3d461e2", "439da8bb9c541d26b0f534b17d75790e252e4d9058561e8907f8690e21cd0616", "c36ebf96573afcb36bb31590d56e8af49502fb159e00fd4a59336f8a450bec8b")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = ProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceProcessEvents" )
| order by Timestamp desc

// Pre-filter both ASIM tables before the union — same pattern as the
// Defender version so the engine can push hash predicates per-side.
let BadHashes = dynamic(["be917cb379b9622f56a4d5ec93bf00c20cb76c6646e5919690d0f7c09c956de2", "c2a7ee6ab9344e1bb13c61dc689d4a946678e0505367cd55c9b43ddee3d461e2", "439da8bb9c541d26b0f534b17d75790e252e4d9058561e8907f8690e21cd0616", "c36ebf96573afcb36bb31590d56e8af49502fb159e00fd4a59336f8a450bec8b"]);
union
    ( ImFileEvent
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where TargetFileSHA256 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileSHA1 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileMD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType,
                  TargetFileName, TargetFilePath, TargetFileSHA256,
                  Cmd = ActingProcessName, Source = "ImFileEvent" ),
    ( ImProcessCreate
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ (BadHashes) or SHA1 in~ (BadHashes) or MD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileName = TargetProcessName, TargetFilePath = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileSHA256 = SHA256,
                  Cmd = TargetProcessCommandLine, Source = "ImProcessCreate" )
| order by TimeGenerated desc

The GitHub Warning Everyone Ignores: 'This Commit Does Not Belong to Any Branch'

StepSecurity
2025-08-09 5 use cases 1 technique 5 kill-chain phases detected

Several popular GitHub Actions have release processes where the release commit does not belong to any branch on the action repository.

CVEsCVE-2025-30066 CVE-2025-30154
ATT&CKT1190

Actions on Objectives
Detected
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration. → Asset exposure — vulnerability matches article CVE(s)
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad). → [LLM] tj-actions / reviewdog imposter-commit SHA referenced on CI/CD runner or developer host
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → Article-specific behavioural hunt — The GitHub Warning Everyone Ignores: 'This Commit Does Not Belong to Any Branch'
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key. → File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking.
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage. → [LLM] tj-actions memdump.py credential-dump payload fetched or executed on CI runner
[LLM] tj-actions / reviewdog imposter-commit SHA referenced on CI/CD runner or developer host Delivery High
Hunts process executions whose command line embeds any of the known imposter commit SHAs that were tagged into tj-actions/changed-files (CVE-2025-30066) or the reviewdog action family (CVE-2025-30154). These SHAs only legitimately appear during the active compromise windows (Mar 11 18:42-20:31 UTC and Mar 14-15 2025); subsequent references mean either a workflow is still pinned to the malicious revision or someone is fetching it. Rationale: Cross-checked against Wiz, CISA AA25-077A, and the GitHub Advisory Database — the five SHAs in the article's IOC bundle are the confirmed imposter commits to which tj-actions/changed-files and the reviewdog actions' tags were pointed during the March 2025 compromise windows. Any process command line still embedding one of these SHAs (git checkout, runner-action invocation, gh api calls) means a pipeline is either still pinned to or fetching the malicious revision. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/advisories/ghsa-mrrh-fwg8-r2c3 • https://www.wiz.io/blog/github-action-tj-actions-changed-files-supply-chain-attack-cve-2025-30066 • https://www.wiz.io/blog/new-github-action-supply-chain-attack-reviewdog-action-setup • https://www.cisa.gov/news-events/alerts/2025/03/18/supply-chain-compromise-third-party-tj-actionschanged-files-cve-2025-30066-and-reviewdogaction • https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/cve-2025-30154
ATT&CKT1195.002 T1199

| order by Timestamp desc

[LLM] tj-actions memdump.py credential-dump payload fetched or executed on CI runner Actions on Objectives High
Hunts the CVE-2025-30066 in-the-wild payload chain: a CI/CD runner (typically Linux ubuntu-latest) pulling https://gist.githubusercontent.com/nikitastupin/30e525b776c409e03c2d6f328f254965/raw/memdump.py and piping it to python3. Catches both the network egress shape and the process-tree shape (curl|python3 with the gist URL in cmdline) so it fires even if the runner blocks one telemetry source. Rationale: The gist URL https://gist.githubusercontent.com/nikitastupin/30e525b776c409e03c2d6f328f254965/raw/memdump.py and the curl|python3 invocation pattern are confirmed by Wiz, Sysdig, and Aqua as the exact in-the-wild payload of CVE-2025-30066 — a Python memory dumper that reads the GitHub Actions Runner.Worker process memory for secrets. The gist ID is unique and unrelated to legitimate GitHub gist traffic, so any reference to it on a runner is malicious. Cross-checked against: • https://www.wiz.io/blog/github-action-tj-actions-changed-files-supply-chain-attack-cve-2025-30066 • https://www.sysdig.com/blog/detecting-and-mitigating-the-tj-actions-changed-files-supply-chain-attack-cve-2025-30066 • https://www.aquasec.com/blog/github-action-tj-actions-changed-files-compromised/ • https://www.cisa.gov/news-events/alerts/2025/03/18/supply-chain-compromise-third-party-tj-actionschanged-files-cve-2025-30066-and-reviewdogaction • https://github.com/advisories/ghsa-mrrh-fwg8-r2c3
ATT&CKT1552.001 T1003 T1567 T1059.006
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes Web Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
let GistId = "30e525b776c409e03c2d6f328f254965";
let ProcessHit = DeviceProcessEvents
  | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
  | where ProcessCommandLine has_any ("memdump.py", GistId, "gist.githubusercontent.com/nikitastupin")
     or InitiatingProcessCommandLine has_any ("memdump.py", GistId, "gist.githubusercontent.com/nikitastupin")
  | extend Signal = "process_cmdline"
  | project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, Signal, FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, FolderPath;
let NetworkHit = DeviceNetworkEvents
  | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
  | where RemoteUrl has GistId or RemoteUrl has "gist.githubusercontent.com/nikitastupin/30e525b776c409e03c2d6f328f254965"
  | extend Signal = "network_egress"
  | project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName=InitiatingProcessAccountName, Signal, FileName=InitiatingProcessFileName, ProcessCommandLine=InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, FolderPath=InitiatingProcessFolderPath;
union ProcessHit, NetworkHit
| order by Timestamp desc
let GistId = "30e525b776c409e03c2d6f328f254965";
let ProcHit = ImProcessCreate
  | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
  | where ActingProcessCommandLine has_any ("memdump.py", GistId, "gist.githubusercontent.com/nikitastupin")
     or TargetProcessCommandLine has_any ("memdump.py", GistId, "gist.githubusercontent.com/nikitastupin")
  | extend Signal = "process_cmdline"
  | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, Signal, ActingProcessName, ActingProcessCommandLine, TargetProcessName, TargetProcessCommandLine;
let WebHit = ImWebSession
  | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
  | where Url has GistId or Url has "gist.githubusercontent.com/nikitastupin/30e525b776c409e03c2d6f328f254965"
  | extend Signal = "web_egress"
  | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername=SrcUsername, Signal, ActingProcessName="", ActingProcessCommandLine=Url, TargetProcessName="", TargetProcessCommandLine=HttpRequestHeaderUserAgent;
union ProcHit, WebHit
| order by TimeGenerated desc

Asset exposure — vulnerability matches article CVE(s) Reconnaissance High
Match vulnerability scanner output to the CVE(s) named in the article.
ATT&CKT1190
Data sourcesVulnerabilities DeviceInfo DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
// TVM tables are daily snapshots — pin a 1-day window so the engine
// partitions on Timestamp instead of scanning the full retention.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2025-30066", "CVE-2025-30154")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where Timestamp > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

// Sentinel has no native TVM table. If MDE→Sentinel streaming is enabled,
// `DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities` is available. Otherwise this UC has
// no Sentinel translation — track via Update / Heartbeat OS-version
// gaps as a coarse approximation.

Match SHA256/SHA1/MD5 named in the article against EDR file/process telemetry.

Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem Endpoint.Processes DeviceFileEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Pre-filter each table BEFORE the union — the engine cannot push
// predicates across `union`, and DeviceFileEvents lacks ProcessCommandLine
// (Initiating only) so column-projection has to differ per side.
union
    ( DeviceFileEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("0e58ed8671d6b60d0890c21b07f8835ace038e67", "fbc2c5ebe64389f297a7808025379f77133f1292", "e1e36574b3af1ddaab74f5e69505d8836bf12f52", "ce4a123414f9fffa959d1f329c4749da83c4bf10", "c17ac4b5c1cb901a7ccddf00ac9722b8e2725345") or SHA1 in~ ("0e58ed8671d6b60d0890c21b07f8835ace038e67", "fbc2c5ebe64389f297a7808025379f77133f1292", "e1e36574b3af1ddaab74f5e69505d8836bf12f52", "ce4a123414f9fffa959d1f329c4749da83c4bf10", "c17ac4b5c1cb901a7ccddf00ac9722b8e2725345") or MD5 in~ ("0e58ed8671d6b60d0890c21b07f8835ace038e67", "fbc2c5ebe64389f297a7808025379f77133f1292", "e1e36574b3af1ddaab74f5e69505d8836bf12f52", "ce4a123414f9fffa959d1f329c4749da83c4bf10", "c17ac4b5c1cb901a7ccddf00ac9722b8e2725345")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceFileEvents" ),
    ( DeviceProcessEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where AccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("0e58ed8671d6b60d0890c21b07f8835ace038e67", "fbc2c5ebe64389f297a7808025379f77133f1292", "e1e36574b3af1ddaab74f5e69505d8836bf12f52", "ce4a123414f9fffa959d1f329c4749da83c4bf10", "c17ac4b5c1cb901a7ccddf00ac9722b8e2725345") or SHA1 in~ ("0e58ed8671d6b60d0890c21b07f8835ace038e67", "fbc2c5ebe64389f297a7808025379f77133f1292", "e1e36574b3af1ddaab74f5e69505d8836bf12f52", "ce4a123414f9fffa959d1f329c4749da83c4bf10", "c17ac4b5c1cb901a7ccddf00ac9722b8e2725345") or MD5 in~ ("0e58ed8671d6b60d0890c21b07f8835ace038e67", "fbc2c5ebe64389f297a7808025379f77133f1292", "e1e36574b3af1ddaab74f5e69505d8836bf12f52", "ce4a123414f9fffa959d1f329c4749da83c4bf10", "c17ac4b5c1cb901a7ccddf00ac9722b8e2725345")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = ProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceProcessEvents" )
| order by Timestamp desc

// Pre-filter both ASIM tables before the union — same pattern as the
// Defender version so the engine can push hash predicates per-side.
let BadHashes = dynamic(["0e58ed8671d6b60d0890c21b07f8835ace038e67", "fbc2c5ebe64389f297a7808025379f77133f1292", "e1e36574b3af1ddaab74f5e69505d8836bf12f52", "ce4a123414f9fffa959d1f329c4749da83c4bf10", "c17ac4b5c1cb901a7ccddf00ac9722b8e2725345"]);
union
    ( ImFileEvent
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where TargetFileSHA256 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileSHA1 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileMD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType,
                  TargetFileName, TargetFilePath, TargetFileSHA256,
                  Cmd = ActingProcessName, Source = "ImFileEvent" ),
    ( ImProcessCreate
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ (BadHashes) or SHA1 in~ (BadHashes) or MD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileName = TargetProcessName, TargetFilePath = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileSHA256 = SHA256,
                  Cmd = TargetProcessCommandLine, Source = "ImProcessCreate" )
| order by TimeGenerated desc

// Article-specific bespoke detection — The GitHub Warning Everyone Ignores: 'This Commit Does Not Belong to Any Branch'
// Hunts the actual binaries / paths / commandline fragments named
// in the article instead of a generic technique-class template.
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where (FileName in~ ("node.js"))
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName,
          FolderPath, ProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine
| order by Timestamp desc

// File-creation events for the named binaries / paths
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where ActionType in ("FileCreated","FileModified")
| where (FileName in~ ("node.js"))
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FolderPath,
          FileName, ActionType, InitiatingProcessFileName,
          InitiatingProcessCommandLine
| order by Timestamp desc
